                              Refer San Francisco let.ter dated September 4, 1947.
                                                                           Port.land, reported
           without                                                       prospe~ting at a
           point in the Cascade l:ountains approJdmately five thousand feet from sea level,
           during the afternoon he noticed a reflection, looked up, and sa\V a disc pro-
           ceeding in a southeasterly direction. Immediately upon sighting this object
           he placed his telescope to his eye and observed t.he disc for approximately
           forty-five to sixty second.. He remarked that it is possible for him to pick
                                                                                                                                                                                            b1L
           up an object at a dist.ance of ten miles with his telescope. At the time the·
           disc was sight.ed by J.\r. , - . it. was banking in the sun, and he observed
           five or six similar objects but. only concentrated on one. He ~lated that
           they did not fly in any particular formation and that he would estimate their
           height. to be about one thousand feet
                                             feet. from where he was standing. He said the
           object was about
           object.     about. thirty feet in diameter.and appeared to have a tail. It made
           no noise.
                                  to 3
                      According t.o            he remained in the vicinity of the Cascades :
                                                                                               I
           for several days and then returned to Portland and noted an article in t.he
           local paper which stat.ed
           sil'lilar object but.
                                                                                         the
                                             effect. that a man in Boise, Idaho, had sighted a b
                                 st.at.ed in effect
                            but that authorities had disclaimed any knowledge of such an
                                                                                                                                                                                              1~
           oeject. He said he communicated with the Army for the sole purpose of attempt-
           ing to add credence
                         ,          to the story furnished by the man in Boise.
              ,     lllllil-alSO related that on the occasion of his sighting the objects
           on lune 24, 1947 he had in his possession a combination compass and watch. He I
           noted particularly that immediately before he sighted the disc the compass      t)~~
           acted very peculiar, the hand waving from one side to the dther, but that this
           condition corrected itself immediately after the discs had passed out of sight.
                   Informant appeared to be a very reliable individual who advised that
          he had been a prospector in the states of Mont.ana,
                                                    Montana, Y/ashington, and Oregon for
          the past forty years.

                                                                                        J~ 7, 1947:

                                 A storm had occurred in Hloenix on the afternoon of July 7, 1947,
                     and the clouds were at approximately 5000 feet, the color of which was grffY
                     and they resembled dense cumulus. A fUteen JDile per hour wind was blowing.
                     At approximately 5 p.m. on that date, Mr.          was walking from his home
                     to the              and he heard a noise resembling a P-80 aircra.ft., apparently
                     coming in a 'West.erly direction. Upon searching the sldes he observed to the
                     northeast 'What appeared to be an odd shaped ship. He described this ship as
                     being possibly 20 to 30 feet in diameter, traveling at an estinated speed of
                     100 JDiles. per hour. It appeared to be making a spiral in a small radius of      fI
                     possibly one-half to three quarters of a mile.           stated the sound which [) {L.
. .-,:-,'
                     he first heard 'Was no longer audible; that he hurriedly procured a 120 Brownie
                     Box Camera from his shop, and as the aircraft completed the first circle he
                     obtained a photograph. As the ship started on the second turn, it was in a
                     northwesterly direction from his place and he again snapped a photograph, 'Which
                     lIIore closely resembled the shape of the ship than photograph #1, this being
                     designated as Exhibit II, as submitted to the Bureau with letter dated August
                     4, 1947, by the Office of Assistant Chief of Staff A-2 Intelligence, Hamilton
                     Field, California. . - - further described the di·rection of the ship by';
                     stating that instead of completing the lefthand downward spiral the ship banked
                     to the right and disappeared into the clouds. He stated that just as it banked,
                     be had snapped the second photograph, Exhibit II.

                    o·         At the last point, 'Which was at the time the ship made this turn, Yr.
                    '-est~ted it to be between 1,000 and 2,000 feet high. He further des- L '1~
                     cribed the appearance of this ship as closely resembling a picture which appaareA' r.-
                     on the front cover of the May, 1947, issue of the Mechanics Illustrated 1&lgazine,
                     ~e only difference noted by Mr.          being that the ship appeared to be fly-
                     ing backward.

                                   Mr • . - . a l s o related that he did not associate the appearance of
                       t,bis ship with the numerous reports of flying discs. He related, however, that
                      after a three JDinute interview with newspaper reporters the~ that appeared          .
                       in the local papers was "blown up" to the extent that he,~ barely recog-
                       nized the information furnished by him. lIr'-'explained that the afore-
                       mentioned dimensions and distances 'IIllre only estimated, pointing out, that his 1 "V
                       previous experience probabl,y asSisted him in making fairly close estimates. HetJ tv
                       related that he had been doing ex:perimentalllork for the past ten years. He
                       also stated he was ElDployed at the Naval Ordnance Laboratory, Washington, D. C.
                       during "ttul early !8rt of World War II and left there the beginning of 1942.
                       Upon his return to Phoenix, he was employed at Falcon Field near Phoenix which
                       was leased by the Bri Ush Government for training purposes. He instructed in
                       instrument training, aircraft identification and gunner] p:-actice. He explained
                       that his hobbies have .to do with science, physiCS, radio, electronics and astronomy •
                     . He produced a model which he had made of a radio controlled airplane. He related
                       that most of his work consists of building transformers, but it is also necessary
                       for him to depend upon his music abili19" for his livlihood.

                           In explaining the results of photographing the ship, Mr.     5
                 stated that he was surprised that the object appeared dark on a light back-
                 ground; that he fully expected that the object would be light on a dark back-
                 ground. He described the light spot which appears in the center of tile object
                 (Exhibit n) as being a green house.           insisted that the ship made no
                 noise that was audible to his ear and he was unable to detect the presence of 0
                                                                                                      '1c;,
                 any propeller. He also related that after the ne.vspaper release over Radio
                 Station KTAR, wherein statements were made that he,~ had stated Army
                 officials were studying tile photographs, and that it was a top secret, he
                 attempted to determine the source of this release and the Radio station finally
                 told him that it was a United Press release froll Washington, D. C.

                                          The following investigation was conducted at Canyonville and
                                 uyrtle Creek, Oregon on August 12, 1947.

                                                                                    Roseburg, Oregon, operator
                                 of the                                    regon, advised that on 8-6-47 at
                                                                        , Oregon,
                                                  :15 PM while instructing a student in a take off at the airport,
                                  he noticed'
                                      noticed'an object east of Myrtle Creek which appeared to be from 5,000 to
                                  8,000 feet in the air. The sky was completely clear and visibility was
                                  excellent. He stated that the object glistened and appeared to be of aluminium
                                  sheeting. Upon noticing it he had immediately taken over the controls of the
.- ....   ',
           ":-
               .                  plane which at that time was at 400 feet, and proceeded East in an attempt to
                                  further observe the object. He advised that the object appeared to be          1_
                                  climbing and traveling East at a high rate of speed which_he estimated on a /.1 7~
                                  computer in his plane as 1,000 miles per hour. He believed the object to be        .
                                  spherical in shape and recalled noticing a darker object to the right the first
                                  time he saw it. He observed no vapor trails nor did he hear any noise fro~ the
 ;b~.·                            object.   Aft~r 6 arching the area for approximately 10 minutes'-"and
    .~~        .'                 his student                returned to the airport and n~de another landing and
 'fd~ )                           take off. In practical y the same position at 400 feet altitude, both ..........
. .. .
 -.            !.                -and         • saw the object in approximately the same position as seen before.
      ;                           He estimated the sphere to be 30 feet in diameter and stated when they first
                                  observed i~ it appeared to be so near he could fly right to it. However, the
                                  o~ject
                                  object sighted the second time disappeared in the same manner as the first had
                                                                                                               had.•
                                    .I
                                  I       It is noted that
                                 Air Corps for about        3t
                                                                    served aa a Lieutenant JG in the U. S. Naval
                                                        years, flying constantly on the Atlantic submarine patrol.
                                                                                                                  h7l
                                 He is a reputable citizen in Douglas County and is reported to be a qualified
                                 pilot.

                                                              Myrtle Creek, Oregon, verified the above information
                                 as furnished by            stating that the first object sighted had been
                                 called to his attention when           took over the controls of the plane at I
                                 about 400 feet following his take off, but that they had both sighted the       ~7~
                                 object on the second take off at the same time.           described the object .
                                 as a, "silver ball or balloon" which he believed to be 8 miles east of t.:yrtle
                                 Creek, traveling East, and climbing very fast until it disappeared in approximataly
                                 45 seconds.           Estimated the sphere as being 50 feet in diameter and
                                 stated in his opinion the second object appeared to climb straight up.


                                                             _ .I''''
                   He a8Tised that he did not observe any evidence of motion such as
                   vapot or trails, etc. and could hear no noise over the noise of his
                   oWn ship.

                                                                       CHICAGO REPORTED SEEING A DISC THREE THIRTY AM
                         TODAY SAILI~G ABOVE LAKE MICHIGAN OFF JACKSON PARK BEACH.                                                                                                   LITTLE'
                         UNAVAILABLE FOR INTERVIEW TODAY.                                                         FATHER IN LAW
